## Existing Games and Mechanics

**Zombie Survival and Sandbox Elements**
- ames like *Project Zomboid* offer extensive sandbox zombie survival experiences, allowing players to customize scenarios, craft items, build shelters, and manage survival needs[1]. However, you always play as a human survivor, not a zombie.
- *Dread Dawn* features open-world survival, crafting, base-building, and a dynamic day/night system, but again, you play as a human[2].
- Many indie zombie games focus on fighting zombies, resource management, and crafting, but none let you play as a zombie with evolving abilities or a disguise system based on blood and body parts[3][4].

**NSFW and Adult Content**
- There are NSFW survival games on platforms like itch.io, including some with zombie themes, but these tend to focus on erotic or adult content rather than deep survival mechanics or playing as a zombie[5].
- Some games blend NSFW elements with survival, but none combine all your requested features, such as marijuana crafting, blood-based evolution, and disguise mechanics.

**Zombie Camouflage and Disguise**
- The concept of disguising yourself as a zombie using blood or body parts has been suggested in communities (inspired by *The Walking Dead*), but is usually limited to temporary camouflage for humans, not a core mechanic for a zombie character[6].

- **Blood Mixing and Evolution:** The mechanic of gathering and mixing different blood types to gain new abilities or manage health and stamina is not present in current games.
- **Disguise and Blending:** Crafting disguises from blood, clothing, and body parts to blend in with humans and zombies is a novel stealth mechanic.
- **Real-Life Crafting (Including Marijuana):** While some survival games allow for crop growing and crafting, few, if any, include marijuana or other real-world crops as a survival or crafting element.
- **Character-Driven Story and Customization:** Allowing players to write their own backstory and influence the narrative based on their choices adds depth rarely seen in zombie games.
- **Decay Mechanic:** The idea that your zombie character decays over time, affecting combat and survival, is a fresh take on the genre.

### **Gathering System (Expanded)**
   - **Resource Collection**: Mechanics for harvesting plants, foraging, and scavenging.
   - **Tools and Equipment**: The use of specific tools like knives, axes, or bare hands to gather resources (wood, plants, food, and zombie parts).
   - **Resource Quality**: Varying qualities of resources depending on location (cannabis buds, herbs, wood, and rare plants could be found in special areas).
   - **Harvesting Cannabis**: Specific system for growing, harvesting, and curing cannabis plants, including seasonal and environmental factors that affect quality.
   - **Crafting Materials**: Gathering materials for crafting items, weapons, or shelter components, with specific gathering mechanics based on tool use (e.g., requiring a specific tool for mining or chopping).
   - **Resource Management**: Managing the amount of resources in inventory with weight and durability limits.
### **Survival and Danger System**
   - **Trap Mechanics**: Crafting and setting traps to catch food, animals, or even survivors/zombies.
   - **Poisonous Plants**: A system for identifying and avoiding harmful plants or mushrooms that can affect health, sanity, or cause hallucinations.
   - **Food Spoilage**: System for perishable items like meat and produce, with spoilage affecting health.
   - **Hazardous Zones**: Areas in the world that contain dangerous hazards (toxic zones, fire zones, etc.) that require special preparation or protection to enter.
### **Skill/Ability/Perk System**
   - **Skill Progression**: Specific skills for the protagonist, like herbalism, crafting, combat, stealth, or communication with animals.
   - **Passive Abilities**: Perks that enhance traits like speed, resistance, damage output, or even cannabis-related effects (e.g., immunity to bloodlust after consuming a special cannabis strain).
   - **Crafting Specializations**: Ability to specialize in certain crafting areas like weapons, shelters, or cannabis-based items for increased effectiveness.
### **Weather System (Expanded)**
   - **Environmental Influence on Gameplay**: Certain types of weather (rain, snow, heatwaves) could directly affect your ability to gather resources, visibility, and survival mechanics.
   - **Hazards Related to Weather**: Impact of extreme weather on combat, visibility, zombie behavior, and other gameplay elements.
### **Combat and Stealth Enhancements**
   - **Zombie and Human AI Tactics**: Advanced AI behaviors for both zombies and humans (e.g., zombies ambushing players, humans hiding from zombies, and reacting to noises).
   - **Melee Combat with Zombie Mutations**: Special combat mechanics based on mutated zombies with unique attacks or weaknesses.
   - **Stealth/Noise Mechanic**: Impact of noise on stealth mechanics, where certain actions or areas may attract zombies or hostile humans.

1. **Blending In with Humans and Zombies:**
   - As a zombie with some remaining human consciousness, players will need to rely heavily on stealth and disguise.
   - Use rotting meat, blood, and clean clothes to hide your zombie features and move through crowds undetected.
   - Interact with both zombies and humans using dialogue options (from an instinctual zombie perspective or as a human trying to hold on to sanity).
2. **Cannabis/Crafting Integration:**
   - Marijuana/Cannabis will be essential for managing bloodlust, slowing decay, and helping with sanity. 
   - Crafting options for players will include:
     - **Marijuana-Related Items:** Grow cannabis, craft edibles, smoke, or create potions that stabilize your character.
     - **Weapons & Tools:** From basic wooden sticks and sharp knives to more advanced crafted gear. Craft items like shelters, rafts, and vehicles as you progress.
     - **Survival Mechanics:** Mix animal blood (or zombie blood) to create stronger versions of yourself, craft traps, and other tools to protect against both humans and zombies.
   
3. **NPC Interactions:**
   - Develop complex relationships with both human survivors and zombies. Depending on your choices, you may form alliances, engage in sexual interactions, or even betray others for survival.
   - The game will be dialogue-heavy, where conversations can lead to gaining new allies, information, or rewards. Choose wisely how you engage with NPCs.
4. **Combat Mechanics:**
   - Start with basic bites and scratches but eventually gain the ability to use makeshift weapons (sticks, rocks, etc.). 
   - Combat will be gritty, with the player learning new moves as they regain consciousness. 
   - The decay mechanic means that fights will get messier and more frantic as time goes on, and maintaining your health becomes increasingly difficult.
5. **Stealth and Disguise Systems:**
   - The key to survival is blending in, and this means you’ll have to cover yourself in blood, find fresh clothing, and create distractions to pass through danger zones undetected.
   - Use environmental elements like abandoned vehicles, alleys, and shadows to hide or escape.
6. **Environmental Design:**
   - **Urban Ruins:** Dilapidated buildings, abandoned subway stations, and city streets littered with looting and chaos.
   - **Natural Areas:** Overgrown forests, beaches, and rivers where you can find resources, craft tools, and grow cannabis.
   - **Day/Night Cycles:** Different threats appear depending on the time of day. Zombies might be more aggressive at night, while humans are more active during the day, creating an ever-present threat of detection.
7. **Character Customization:**
   - **Zombie Customization:** Customize your character’s appearance, from body shape and tattoos to personality traits. 
   - You can also customize your look and actions depending on your desired level of “human” consciousness vs. zombie instincts.
8. **Rehabilitation or Acceptance:**
   - Depending on the choices you make, you can either attempt to find a cure or simply embrace your new existence as a zombie. The storyline will allow for both outcomes, with branching paths based on your survival tactics and moral decisions.
9. **NSFW Content:**
   - Integrate mature themes naturally into the narrative, such as the protagonist’s struggles with intimacy and human connections, both with humans and fellow zombies.
   - You can make adult decisions that affect gameplay and the storyline, but the overall tone will still tie into the game’s dark humor, survival, and emotional struggle.
### Emotional Engagement:
The idea of a zombie protagonist still holding on to a sense of self allows for deep emotional exploration, from grappling with the loss of humanity to finding solace in old habits (like marijuana). The player will feel a connection to the character, balancing the horror with the bizarre, yet human-like, actions they take.
### Challenges to Address:
- **Narrative Balance:** The combination of NSFW content with the horror and survival elements needs to be carefully managed to ensure the tone is consistent and engaging.
- **Ethical Sensitivity:** Given the mature content, it’s essential to design gameplay that respects boundaries while still delivering on the promised NSFW elements. 
- **Crafting and Progression:** Make sure the crafting system feels rewarding, with each crafted item feeling significant in the game’s survival or narrative aspects.

#### **1. Exploration**
- **World Design**: Encourage players to explore a dynamic, semi-open world filled with hidden secrets, resources, and dangers.
- **Environment Diversity**: Include different biomes (urban ruins, dense forests, swamps, labs, etc.), each with unique loot, enemies, and challenges.
- **Day-Night Cycle**: Introduce different threats depending on the time of day. For example:
  - Zombies become more aggressive at night.
  - Survivors roam during the day and may pose a threat or ally opportunity.  


---


#### **2. Scavenging**
- **Resource Gathering**:
  - Collect cannabis plants, crafting materials (herbs, alcohol, fabric), and technology parts from the environment.
  - Search abandoned buildings, vehicles, and corpses for essential items like tools, food, or ammunition.
- **Inventory Management**:
  - Limited inventory forces players to prioritize resources (e.g., choosing between cannabis plants and medical supplies).
- **Risk-Reward System**: Add dangers while scavenging (e.g., zombie ambushes, traps, or rival survivors).  


---


#### **3. Crafting**
- **Core Items**:
  - **Cannabis Concoctions**: Create items to suppress Phoenix’s bloodlust, enhance her abilities, or heal wounds.
  - **Weapons**: Craft makeshift weapons like nail-bats, Molotovs, or electrified tools.
  - **Survival Gear**: Build traps, protective clothing, or tools for exploration.
- **Workbench Mechanics**:
  - Require players to find or build workbenches in safe zones to craft complex items.
  - Introduce recipes gradually, rewarding exploration and progression.
  
---


#### **4. Managing Bloodlust (Unique Mechanic)**
- **Phoenix’s Condition**:
  - A constantly depleting "Bloodlust Meter" represents her growing urge to feed.  
  - Consuming crafted cannabis items slows or resets the meter, keeping her sane.
- **Consequences**:
  - If the bloodlust meter maxes out, Phoenix loses control and attacks anything nearby, causing moral dilemmas and risking alliances.
  - Finding unique cannabis strains with special effects (e.g., prolonged calm or enhanced combat abilities) adds variety.
  
---


#### **5. Story Progression**
- **Dynamic Narrative**:
  - Include branching storylines based on player decisions (e.g., sparing or killing survivors, pursuing a cure, or embracing her zombie nature).
  - Progression through main quests (e.g., uncovering what happened at the lab) and side quests from survivors.
- **Emotional Themes**:
  - Show Phoenix’s struggle with identity through dialogue, flashbacks, and moral decisions.


---


### **Size and Scope of the Open-World Sandbox**
The world should be large enough to encourage exploration but manageable for a 2D sandbox. Here’s a possible layout:


#### **World Size**
- **Semi-Open World**:
  - Divide the world into interconnected regions (zones), each unlocked as players progress. This keeps exploration exciting and manageable.
- **Map Size**: Roughly **6–8 regions**, each around 1–2 km² in scale.  
  - Example Regions:  
    1. **Urban Ruins** (City streets, abandoned shops, and skyscrapers).  
    2. **Forested Suburbs** (Hidden cabins, overgrown backyards, and survivor camps).  
    3. **Industrial Zone** (Warehouses, factories, and tunnels).  
    4. **Abandoned Lab Facility** (Key to uncovering Phoenix’s backstory).  
    5. **Swamp Lands** (Difficult terrain with rare resources and dangerous enemies).  
    6. **Military Outpost** (High-risk, high-reward scavenging zone).  


---


#### **Points of Interest (POIs)**
1. **Safehouses**:
   - Scattered across the map as small, secure zones where players can save, craft, or recover.
   - Each safehouse requires effort to secure (e.g., barricading doors, setting traps).
   
2. **Survivor Camps**:
   - Hubs for side quests and trading.
   - Some camps may distrust Phoenix (due to her zombie nature), forcing players to earn their trust.
   
3. **Abandoned Buildings**:
   - Randomized interiors to keep exploration unpredictable.
   - Include notes, logs, or personal belongings to build lore and add emotional weight.
   
4. **High-Risk Zones**:
   - Areas with rare loot but deadly enemies (e.g., mutant zombies, toxic zones).
   - Examples: A quarantined hospital, a collapsed metro station, or the lab’s secure vault.  


5. **Easter Eggs and Lore**:
   - Hidden details about Phoenix’s past, the outbreak, and the collapse of civilization.



Phoenix’s abilities, upgrades, and personal growth arc will intertwine with her journey as a zombie retaining her consciousness. Her skills and powers evolve as she adapts to her undead condition, manages her bloodlust, and balances her humanity with her new nature.


---


### **Phoenix’s Core Abilities**
Her abilities will focus on survival, combat, exploration, and emotional control.  


#### **1. Survival Abilities**
- **Bloodlust Management**:
  - Ability to temporarily suppress her urges through cannabis-based items, calming activities, or feeding.
  - Bloodlust Meter mechanics dictate her state:
    - **Calm**: Fully lucid, focused, and stealthy.
    - **Hungry**: Combat-focused, with increased aggression and physical strength.
    - **Uncontrolled**: Fully consumed by her zombie instincts, attacking friend and foe alike.
- **Zombie Resilience**:
  - Immunity to disease and toxins that would harm humans.
  - Slow health regeneration over time, boosted by cannabis concoctions.


#### **2. Combat Abilities**
- **Enhanced Strength**:  
  - As a zombie, Phoenix has superior strength compared to humans, allowing her to:
    - Rip through barricades, throw heavy objects, or overpower enemies in melee.
  - Base strength grows with upgrades, enabling her to deal massive damage.
- **Feral Instincts**:  
  - Ability to detect living or undead creatures by smell or sound, highlighting nearby threats.
  - Upgrades improve range and accuracy, and later allow her to identify friend from foe.
- **Claw Attacks**:  
  - Her sharp nails can be used as weapons. Upgrades make them deadlier, allowing:
    - Quick slashes for crowd control.
    - A devastating charged attack that stuns enemies.


#### **3. Exploration Abilities**
- **Wall Climbing**:
  - Early in the game, Phoenix gains the ability to climb walls, fences, or trees to escape or reach high areas.
  - Upgrades increase climbing speed and stamina.
- **Enhanced Vision**:
  - Zombie eyes adapt to darkness, giving her night vision.  
  - Upgrades enhance her ability to spot hidden objects or threats in dim environments.
- **Silent Movement**:
  - Phoenix can learn to suppress the sound of her footsteps.
  - Stealth-based upgrades reduce noise further, allowing her to sneak past zombies or humans.


---


### **Upgrades and Progression System**
Upgrades can be earned through exploration, crafting, completing quests, or leveling up.  


#### **1. Combat Upgrades**
- **Feral Fury**: Increases her attack speed and damage during combat.
- **Limb Targeting**: Adds the ability to disable enemy limbs (e.g., sever arms or break legs).  
- **Weapon Proficiency**: Allows her to handle improvised weapons more efficiently.


#### **2. Bloodlust Control Upgrades**
- **Meditative State**:
  - Prolonged suppression of bloodlust through cannabis use and calming activities.
  - At higher levels, Phoenix can fully regain control in chaotic situations.
- **Feeding Efficiency**:
  - Reduces the amount of flesh or cannabis items needed to reset the bloodlust meter.


#### **3. Mobility Upgrades**
- **Leap Distance**: Enhances her jump range, making it easier to evade enemies or traverse large gaps.
- **Dash Ability**: Adds a short burst of speed to evade attacks or escape dangerous situations.
- **Improvised Grappling**: Craft a grappling hook from scavenged items to reach even higher areas.


#### **4. Passive Upgrades**
- **Pain Tolerance**:
  - Reduces damage taken from attacks.
  - At higher levels, adds temporary immunity to stagger or knockback effects.
- **Adaptation**:
  - Phoenix becomes more resistant to environmental hazards (e.g., fire, radiation, or toxic gas).
- **Cannabis Mastery**:
  - Improved effects of crafted items (e.g., faster healing, extended calm state, or temporary strength boosts).


---


### **Phoenix’s Personal Growth Arc**
Phoenix’s story is driven by her internal battle between succumbing to her zombie instincts and maintaining her humanity.  


#### **1. Act 1: Awareness and Survival**
- **Key Themes**:
  - Phoenix awakens as a zombie, horrified by what she has become.
  - Her initial goal is simple survival, learning to suppress her bloodlust and avoid attacks from survivors and zombies.
- **Core Conflict**:
  - Phoenix begins to struggle with the morality of feeding on human flesh to survive.
  - Early interactions with survivors shape her views on humanity—whether to save or harm them.


---


#### **2. Act 2: Understanding and Power**
- **Key Themes**:
  - Phoenix delves into the mystery of the outbreak and her transformation.  
  - She starts to embrace her zombie abilities, using them to protect herself and others.
  - As she grows stronger, she questions whether she should search for a cure or embrace her new identity.
- **Core Conflict**:
  - Phoenix becomes increasingly tempted by her bloodlust. Choices about feeding or suppressing her urges affect her appearance and mental state.
  - Moral dilemmas arise, like choosing to save a survivor at the cost of feeding her bloodlust.


---


#### **3. Act 3: Acceptance or Transformation**
- **Key Themes**:
  - The story culminates in Phoenix making a decision about her future:
    1. **Seek Redemption**: Find a cure, restore her humanity, and fight to rebuild the world.
    2. **Embrace Her Nature**: Accept her zombie self, becoming a bridge between humans and the undead.
- **Core Conflict**:
  - The final act explores Phoenix’s acceptance of who she has become, whether as a zombie or as a human.  
  - Player decisions influence whether Phoenix remains a symbol of hope or a force of chaos.



### **2. Choose Technology Stack**
- **Game Engine or Framework**:
  - If sticking to no engine, plan out the **core programming languages** (e.g., **C++, Python, or Rust**) and libraries for 2D rendering (like SDL2 or SFML).  
  - Alternatively, consider lightweight engines like **Godot** or **PhaserJS** (great for 2D browser games).  
- **Tools**:
  - **Graphics**: Aseprite or Photoshop for sprite creation.  
  - **Audio**: Audacity for sound effects, or tools like FMOD or Wwise for dynamic sound design.  
  - **Animation**: Spine 2D or DragonBones for smooth 2D animations.  


---


### **1. Basic Movement**
**Objective**: Create a simple map where Phoenix can walk, run, climb, and interact with objects.


#### **Steps**:
- **Map Design**:
  - Create a small test map with varied terrain (flat ground, obstacles, walls to climb, objects to interact with).
  - Use placeholder assets for Phoenix, the terrain, and interactive objects.
- **Movement Implementation**:
  - Program Phoenix’s basic movement:
    - **Walk**: Arrow keys/WASD for movement.
    - **Run**: Add a sprint mechanic (Shift to run, limited by stamina).
    - **Climb**: Introduce a climbing mechanic (E or Space near walls).
  - Add smooth animations for transitions between idle, walking, running, and climbing states.
- **Interaction System**:
  - Set up an "interact" key (e.g., F) to trigger actions like opening doors or picking up items.
  - Display prompts when near interactable objects (e.g., “Press F to pick up”).


---


### **2. Combat System**
**Objective**: Develop basic melee and ranged combat mechanics.


#### **Steps**:
- **Melee Combat**:
  - Implement a simple attack button (e.g., left mouse or a key like Q).
  - Create an attack animation with hit detection:
    - Use raycasting or colliders to detect if the attack hits enemies within range.
  - Add basic damage calculation (e.g., enemies take X damage per hit).
- **Ranged Combat**:
  - Add a ranged attack ability (e.g., throw a rock or shoot an improvised weapon).
  - Implement aiming (mouse or joystick) and a projectile system:
    - Use Unity’s Rigidbody physics for projectiles or an arc system.
- **Enemy AI**:
  - Prototype a simple zombie enemy with basic behavior:
    - Wander when idle.
    - Chase Phoenix when detected.
    - Attack when within range.


---


### **3. Bloodlust Mechanic**
**Objective**: Prototype Phoenix’s bloodlust system where she must consume cannabis to suppress her urges.


#### **Steps**:
- **Bloodlust Meter**:
  - Display a meter on the screen that represents Phoenix’s current bloodlust state (e.g., calm → hungry → uncontrolled).
  - Set the meter to increase over time or when specific events occur (e.g., combat or taking damage).
- **Cannabis Suppression**:
  - Add a consumable cannabis item that decreases the meter when used.
  - Add effects when the meter changes:
    - **Calm**: Regular animations and gameplay.
    - **Hungry**: Aggressive animations, increased damage, but slower crafting speed.
    - **Uncontrolled**: Loss of player control, Phoenix attacks everyone nearby.
- **Integration**:
  - Place cannabis items on the map for Phoenix to pick up.
  - Set up a crafting station where Phoenix can make suppressants (see crafting system below).


---


### **4. Crafting System**
**Objective**: Create a simple crafting interface for Phoenix to create cannabis-based items.


#### **Steps**:
- **Resource Collection**:
  - Place crafting materials (e.g., cannabis plants, herbs, bottles) on the map for Phoenix to collect.
  - Add a simple inventory system to track collected items.
- **Crafting Interface**:
  - Build a basic crafting menu (e.g., accessed by pressing a button like Tab or C).
  - Display recipes for cannabis-based items like:
    - **Healing Salve**: Restores health.
    - **Bloodlust Suppressant**: Reduces the bloodlust meter.
    - **Enhancement Concoction**: Temporarily boosts attack or speed.
- **Crafting Process**:
  - Require players to select a recipe and have the necessary materials to craft an item.
  - Add a short crafting animation or timer for realism.
- **Usage**:
  - Enable crafted items to be used directly from the inventory.


---


### **Testing and Iteration**
Once the prototypes are ready, test each system individually:
- Ensure movement is smooth and responsive.
- Test combat for balance and ensure animations sync correctly with actions.
- Check the bloodlust mechanic for pacing and urgency.
- Refine crafting by balancing resource availability and crafting times.

She was a former weed-smoking computer scientist who worked for a Bio-Viral-Agro-Pharma-Tech conglomerate researching the resurrection of the dead. After a failed experiment, she is turned into a zombie. However, she struggles with maintaining her humanity and desires to either find a cure or learn to live peacefully as an undead. Phoenix uses marijuana to manage her bloodlust and retain her humanity.

**Blood, Lust & Leftovers: A Zombie’s Guide to Survival & Seduction**  
**Definitive Complete Game Design Document**  
**Single Consolidated Source of Truth**

---

### 1. Game Overview

**Title:** Blood, Lust & Leftovers: A Zombie’s Guide to Survival & Seduction  

**Genre:** 2D Open-World Sandbox Survival-Crafting with deep Stealth, Character-Driven Narrative, Systemic Progression, and fully Toggleable Mature/NSFW content.

**Core Premise:**  
You play as Phoenix, a newly turned, fully conscious zombie. You retain your intelligence, memories, personality, and desires while dealing with bloodlust, physical decay, and the need to blend into both human and zombie populations. Cannabis is a core survival resource for managing bloodlust and decay. Blood mixing provides temporary and permanent power. The central question of the game is: what do you keep, and what do you become, when you are no longer fully human but refuse to be mindless?

**Unique Selling Points:**
- Playable as a conscious zombie (not a human survivor)
- Blood-mixing progression and identity system
- Full cannabis cultivation and consumption loop tied to core meters
- Dual-population disguise and stealth
- Primitive multi-use tool philosophy (Multi-Stick)
- Deep optional romance and intimacy
- Meaningful branching endings based on how the player handles identity

**Tone:** Dark observational humor, intimate body horror, quiet melancholy, sudden tenderness, and hard-won clarity.

**Target Audience:** Mature (18+) players who want systemic depth and emotional weight in the zombie survival genre.

**Platforms:** PC (Steam primary), Browser (HTML5), Android (with strong content filters).  
**Recommended Engine:** Godot 4.

---

### 2. Story & Characters

**Protagonist – Phoenix**  
Fully customizable. Default backstory: mid-level scientist/engineer at a Bio-Viral-Agro-Pharma-Tech conglomerate that tolerated and supplied her heavy cannabis use. A resurrection experiment killed her and returned her with continuity of consciousness. Players can alter memory fragments and personality leanings in the creator; these choices affect internal monologue and early dialogue.

**Internal Voices:**  
Four competing internal voices (Scientist, Survivor, Hungry One, Witness) that strengthen or weaken based on meters and mutations, creating a living internal conflict.

**Key Characters:**
- **Marcus** — Former EMT. Pragmatic, consistency-focused. Trust is earned through observable reliability and refusal to feed on humans in his presence. Romance path is about mutual recognition of damage.
- **Dr. Lena Voss** — Senior researcher from the original project. Intellectual guilt. Strongest natural ally for the Cure path. Romance is slow and conversational.
- **Riven** — Early conscious undead who has already chosen embrace. Charismatic and philosophically ruthless. Mentor, mirror, and potential lover. Highest affinity for the Embrace path.

**Opening Sequence (Polished Textual Script):**  
Phoenix stumbles out of a ruined lab into a chaotic dusk street. Her body feels wrong. A zombie looks at her and does not attack. A human screams and flees.  
Internal monologue rises in panic: “I’m still thinking. Full sentences. Zombies don’t get internal monologues…”  
Bloodlust spikes. She collapses.  
Title card.  
She wakes in an alley. A half-smoked joint lies nearby. The Bloodlust meter appears. Smoking it drops the meter. The player gains control.

**Act Structure:**
- **Act 1 – Still Thinking:** Learn all core systems, secure shelter, establish cannabis supply, first blood mixtures, meet Marcus, first recognition from another conscious zombie, reach Lab perimeter.
- **Act 2 – What Still Remains:** Infiltrate the Lab Complex, meet Riven and Lena, permanent mutation choices, large-scale growing, loyalty tests, decide the fate of the core research data.
- **Act 3 – What We Choose to Keep:** Six primary endings + one hidden ending (Full Restoration, Partial Cure, The Treaty, The Watcher, Apex, Solitary God, and the hidden “Still Thinking” quiet garden ending). Each has a full cinematic, unique final body state, and epilogue consequences for all major characters.

---

### 3. Complete Systems

**Core Meters:**  
Bloodlust (Pressure + Spike subtypes, tiered Uncontrolled states), Decay (Surface + Deep layers), Sanity (affects monologue reliability and sensory accuracy).

**Cannabis System:**  
Full seed-to-smoke cycle with multiple strains, hidden genetics, quality spectrum, optional micro-management, smoke/edible/resin methods, hemp byproducts, soft dependency, and faction cultural knowledge.

**Blood Alchemy:**  
Multiple blood types (human fresh/stale, zombie variants, animals, Lab-Grade). Up to 3-ingredient mixes. Effect categories: Metabolic, Sensory, Structural, Cognitive, Social, Mutagenic. External application for disguise. Stabilizers for extended/permanent effects. Limited permanent mutation slots. Blood as social currency among conscious undead.

**Stealth & Disguise:**  
Three-layer detection (visual, auditory, olfactory). Continuous disguise value. Zombie Camouflage vs Human Camouflage. Active maintenance required. Advanced techniques include controlled leakage and companion scent masking.

**Zombie Combat System (Detailed):**  
Personal, noisy, and state-expressive.  
Unarmed toolkit: Light Claw, Heavy Claw/Smash, Bite/Grab (enables feeding), Shove/Throw, Bloodlust-gated Lunge.  
Weapon families synergize with active blood mixtures. Multi-Stick is the philosophical center.  
Feeding is a deliberate, multi-second, high-consequence action.  
Bloodlust states (Calm / Hungry / Uncontrolled tiers) alter control, damage, and behavior.  
Permanent mutations unlock new combat properties.  
Environmental tools and animal companions are first-class options. Highest skill often means refusing pure melee.

**Gathering:**  
Hand, tool-assisted, and scavenging. Quality varies by biome, weather, time, and tools. Knowledge system for plants, blood quality, and animal state. Risk layers include noise and competing scavengers.

**Crafting:**  
Primitive multi-use philosophy centered on the Multi-Stick. Workbench progression. Recipe discovery through experimentation, flashbacks, and NPCs. Quality and customization bias (durability, damage, weight, stealth).

**Building & Territory:**  
Modular progression from lean-to to underground/cave conversion. Functional stations (storage, workbench, grow beds, drying racks, animal pens, bicycle workshop). Comfort/security/production scores. Territory pressure affects faction behavior. Strong aesthetic expression support.

**Companions & Animals:**  
Taming via approach, offerings, Bloodlust, and disguise. Individual temperament and memory. Roles: Guard, Scout, Hauler, Companion, Mount. Breeding possible. Human and conscious-undead companions have schedules. Death or betrayal carries permanent weight.

**Romance, Relationship & NSFW:**  
Dual meters (Trust + Affinity). Stages up to Intimate. Fully optional and gated. Intimacy provides mechanical benefits and story flags. Granular toggles (Off / Suggestive / Explicit for text and animation). Complete disable option. NPCs remember specific events. Consequential breakup/repair paths. Example Intimate dialogue trees exist for Marcus, Lena, and Riven.

**Multi-Stick:**  
Single crafted object used as weapon, digging tool, fishing spear, grass cutter, walking staff, pry bar, and crafting base. Remains relevant the entire game through upgrades and techniques.

**World & Ecology:**  
Semi-open world of interconnected regions (City Center, Suburban Fringe/Forest, Coastal, Deep Forest, Farmlands, Industrial, Mountains, Lab Complex). Full day/night and dynamic weather. Persistent simulation for crops, animals, and region pressure. Player actions permanently shift local ecosystems and faction behavior.

**AI & Enemies:**  
Detailed sensory models and state machines for Walkers, Runners, Brutes, Spitters, Lab Mutants, lone scavengers, organized human patrols, and wildlife. Global modifiers from player Bloodlust and disguise. Readable group coordination. Faction reputation tracking.

**Progression:**  
Skills in Stealth, Combat, Herbalism/Cannabis, Blood Alchemy, Animal Handling, Crafting, Social. Limited permanent mutation slots. Story and relationship flags open or close options.

---

### 4. Technical Foundation

- Modular component-based architecture (Bloodlust, Decay, Disguise, Cannabis, Relationship, Mutation components).  
- Fully data-driven Resources for blood, strains, items, effects, and dialogue.  
- Robust versioned save system (multiple manual slots + autosave + quicksave).  
- Comprehensive accessibility (remapping, text scaling, colorblind modes, screen reader, combat assists, granular NSFW toggles including full romance disable).  
- Achievement system for mechanical and narrative feats.  
- Exposed modding surface.  
- Recommended Godot scene hierarchy and core script stubs previously defined.

---

### 5. Prioritized Feature Backlog & MVP

**MVP (Playable Vertical Slice):**  
One region, full movement + climbing, Bloodlust/Decay/Stamina meters, basic cannabis use, simple blood collection and 3–4 mixes, Multi-Stick with multiple uses, basic disguise, 2–3 enemy types, basic crafting and shelter, inventory, save system, opening sequence, NSFW toggle (even if content is minimal), day/night cycle.

**Priority 1:** Full cannabis grow cycle, expanded blood mixing, animal taming (small animals), multiple regions + safehouses, dialogue system + key NPCs, building upgrades, bicycle transport.

**Priority 2:** Full story Acts 1–2, relationship/NSFW framework, advanced combat and mutations, rafting, weather impact, more animals.

**Priority 3:** Multiple endings, New Game+ considerations, full audio, extensive accessibility polish, mod support.

---

### 6. Emotional & Philosophical Core

Every system exists to make the player feel the weight of small decisions accumulating into an identity.  
The blood you mix, the strains you grow, the people you trust or feed on, the way you fight or refuse to fight, the moments you suppress or indulge your hunger — these are authorship tools.

The endings do not rank the player.  
They recognize the player.

### 1. Expanded Act 2 (Replace existing Act 2 text)

**Act 2 – What Still Remains (Fully Expanded)**

Act 2 is the longest and most systemic act. It begins the moment the player commits to entering the greater Lab Complex region and ends with the irreversible choice about the core research data.

**2.1 – The Approach**  
- Travel through either the Industrial Zone or the Deep Forest (player choice affects available resources and encounters).  
- New enemy types appear: toxic-spitters, armored brutes, and the first organized human hunting parties that specifically target “talkers.”  
- First major blood-mixing breakthrough tutorial (player unlocks longer-duration and semi-permanent mixtures).  
- Optional side crisis: a cannabis garden the player planted in Act 1 is either thriving, failing, or has been discovered by scavengers.

**2.2 – Outer Campus**  
- Large explorable research campus with multiple wings.  
- Audio logs and terminal entries reveal Phoenix was Subject 17 and that at least eleven other subjects retained partial or full continuity.  
- First meeting with **Riven** (conscious zombie) — can be hostile, curious, or immediately flirtatious depending on player’s current mutation load and Bloodlust state.  
- First meeting with **Dr. Lena Voss** (human) — she recognizes Phoenix and has a crisis of her own.

**2.3 – Power & Root**  
- Player can convert a greenhouse wing into a high-efficiency grow operation (new advanced cannabis mechanics unlock).  
- Lab-Grade blood samples become available.  
- First permanent mutation is offered (player must accept or reject; refusal is remembered by Riven and Lena).  
- Relationship pressure increases: Marcus may reappear with a request that forces a public loyalty test.

**2.4 – Fractured Loyalties**  
- Three major branching crises occur in parallel (player can only fully resolve two):  
  1. A survivor group demands Phoenix prove she is “safe.”  
  2. A group of conscious undead asks her to help eliminate a human outpost.  
  3. Lena offers a partial temporary suppressant in exchange for helping her recover specific data.  
- Romance paths can advance to Intimate stage during this section if meters are high enough.

**2.5 – The Heart**  
- Infiltration of the central research spire.  
- Major set-piece: a multi-stage encounter that can be solved through pure combat, stealth + disguise, blood-powered abilities, or social manipulation.  
- Climax choice (irreversible and heavily weighted toward endings):  
  - Destroy the core data and samples.  
  - Secure the data for a potential cure.  
  - Use the data and samples to force a major personal evolution.  

Act 2 ends with a short cinematic that changes based on the choice and the player’s dominant playstyle (suppression-heavy, balanced, or mutation-heavy).

---

### 2. Blood Mixing – Full Mechanical & Narrative Explanation  
**(Insert as 3.4.1)**

Blood Mixing is both a power system and an identity system.

**How it works mechanically:**  
- Blood is extracted from living or freshly dead targets using a basic extraction tool (later upgraded).  
- Freshness matters: Fresh blood gives stronger effects but spoils faster.  
- Players combine up to three blood types at a workbench (or two via quick-mix in the field).  
- A fourth “Stabilizer” slot exists for high-grade cannabis resin or rare lab chemicals.  
- Each mixture produces a temporary 
effect package (8–25 minutes baseline) and contributes hidden “resonance” toward permanent mutations.  
- Permanent mutations occupy limited slots (starting with 1, expandable to 4). Once locked in, they change appearance, available combat options, dialogue reactions, and disguise baselines permanently.

**Narrative weight:**  
Every time the player mixes blood they are literally rewriting what Phoenix is. Heavy use of human blood makes her appear and smell more human (better Human Camouflage) but increases internal conflict. Heavy use of zombie or predator blood makes her more powerful and more obviously other. Conscious undead NPCs can smell recent mixtures and will comment on them. Human NPCs react to visible mutation stage.

**Risk & Expression:**  
Over-reliance on the same blood type creates tolerance (diminishing returns) and eventually forced mutation risk. The system is designed so that pure optimization is possible but narratively costly — the most powerful builds are also the most socially isolating.

---

### 3. NSFW & Romance System – Full Design Explanation  
**(Insert as 3.11.1)**

The NSFW and romance systems are built on the same philosophy as the rest of the game: optional, systemic, and identity-relevant.

**Structure:**  
- Two independent meters per romance-capable NPC: Trust (reliability and safety) and Affinity (emotional/physical draw).  
- Intimacy is only available at the Intimate stage and only if the player has the NSFW setting enabled.  
- Content is split into three tiers controlled independently:  
  - Off — all romantic and sexual content removed or faded to black.  
  - Suggestive — emotional intimacy, kissing, fade-to-black, implied scenes.  
  - Explicit — full animated or detailed descriptive scenes (player choice of perspective and intensity).  

**Mechanical Integration:**  
Successful intimacy provides one of the strongest temporary Bloodlust reductions in the game, a Sanity buffer, and a short-term stat boost. It also writes permanent story flags that alter ending epilogues and certain Act 3 dialogue.

**Narrative Integration:**  
Intimacy is never required for any ending or major progression. It is treated as one possible way characters seek connection, comfort, or distraction in a collapsing world. Each romanceable character has distinct boundaries and desires that can conflict with the player’s current mutation level or feeding habits. Breaking those boundaries can destroy the relationship permanently.

**Design Safeguards:**  
- Clear, repeated consent framing in dialogue.  
- Easy global toggle + per-scene skip.  
- No sexual content involving non-conscious zombies or non-consenting characters.  
- Complete disable option that removes all romance meters and related dialogue if desired.

---

### 4. New Section 3.16 – Additional Systems & Advanced Mechanics  
**(Add at the end of Section 3)**

**3.16.1 Scent & Memory Trail System**  
Phoenix leaves a faint scent trail that persists for a short time. High Bloodlust or recent feeding makes the trail stronger and detectable by both zombies and certain animals. Players can deliberately mask or broadcast scent.

**3.16.2 Dynamic Reputation Layers**  
Three separate reputation tracks: Human Survivor Factions, Conscious Undead Network, and Wildlife Wariness. Actions affect all three differently. Reputation alters available dialogue, prices, ambush frequency, and ending availability.

**3.16.3 Advanced Multi-Stick Techniques**  
Late-game skill tree unlocks special Multi-Stick arts: vaulting strikes, trip techniques, improvised spear throws, and the ability to plant the stick as a temporary distraction or trap anchor.

**3.16.4 Sleep & Dream System**  
Sleeping in secure shelters can trigger dreams that are either restorative, prophetic (gameplay hints), or nightmarish (temporary Sanity damage and possible flashbacks). Cannabis strain and recent blood mixtures influence dream type.

**3.16.5 Tool Degradation & Repair Ecology**  
All tools and weapons have durability. Repair requires specific materials and knowledge. Players can specialize in scavenged high-quality tools or mass-produced disposable ones.

**3.16.6 Emergency Identity Moments**  
Rare scripted or systemic events force the player into situations where disguise is impossible and they must choose how to reveal (or weaponize) their nature. These moments generate strong story flags.


**The Other Subjects (Subject 17 = Phoenix)**

In the Lab Complex audio logs, terminal entries, and scattered personal effects, the player learns that Phoenix was designated **Subject 17**. At least eleven other subjects retained partial or full continuity of consciousness after the failed resurrection event. Their fates and current states vary widely. Here is the complete canonical list used in the game:

| Subject | Name / Designation | Continuity Level | Current Status | Personality / Role | Path Alignment |
|---------|---------------------|------------------|----------------|---------------------|----------------|
| 03      | Dr. Elias Kane     | High            | Deceased (suicide) | Former lead ethicist. Left detailed confessional logs. | Cure (tragic) |
| 06      | “Marrow”           | High            | Active (hostile) | Fully embraced predation. Leads a small feral conscious pack. | Embrace |
| 08      | Subject 08         | Partial         | Feral / Lost    | Retained only fragments. Wanders the lower lab levels repeating the same three sentences. | None (horror element) |
| 09      | Lila Vey           | High            | Active          | Former lab technician. Trying to live quietly among humans using heavy disguise and suppressants. | Coexistence / Cure |
| 11      | “Rack”             | Medium-High     | Active          | Brutal pragmatist. Runs a blood-trading operation with both humans and undead. | Coexistence (dark) |
| 12      | Subject 12         | Low-Partial     | Contained       | Locked in a sealed observation room. Still aware but almost completely nonverbal. | None (can be freed) |
| 14      | Harlan Crowe       | High            | Active          | Former security chief. Extremely disciplined. Believes conscious undead must police their own. | Coexistence |
| 15      | “Iris”             | High            | Active          | Artist / former botanist. Obsessed with growing cannabis strains that affect undead differently. Potential grow-op ally. | Coexistence |
| 16      | Subject 16         | Medium          | Missing         | Escaped early. Only rumors and one blurred security photo exist. | Unknown (mystery) |
| 18      | Riven              | Very High       | Active (major NPC) | One of the most stable and articulate. Already chosen Embrace. | Embrace |
| 19      | Dr. Sofia Nguyen   | High            | Active (hidden) | Junior researcher who was infected late. Working on a real cure in secret. | Cure |

**Key Notes for Implementation:**

- **Riven (18)** is the only one who is a full major romance/companion NPC from the start of Act 2.
- **Lila Vey (09)** and **Iris (15)** can become secondary allies or romance options if the player invests in them.
- **Marrow (06)** serves as a recurring hostile conscious-undead antagonist and ideological foil.
- **Harlan Crowe (14)** can become a powerful ally for a strict “rules-based” Coexistence run.
- **Dr. Sofia Nguyen (19)** is the hidden true-cure contact for the purest Cure ending (A1).
- Subjects 08 and 12 are primarily atmospheric/horror encounters that reinforce how rare and fragile full continuity is.
- Subject 16 is left deliberately mysterious so players can project or so future content can expand on them.


**The Other Subjects (Expanded List)**  
Phoenix is **Subject 17**.  
There are **14 other subjects** who retained partial or full continuity of consciousness.

Here is the complete canonical list:

### 3.17.1 Audio & Music System

**Design Goal:** Audio is a primary information channel and emotional driver, not just atmosphere.

**Dynamic Music System**
- Four horizontal layers that cross-fade based on real-time state:
  1. Exploration / Calm (low Bloodlust, safe area, strong disguise)
  2. Tension (elevated Bloodlust, nearby threats, weak disguise)
  3. Combat / Chase
  4. Uncontrolled / Horror (plays only during Uncontrolled Bloodlust or extreme Sanity loss)
- Vertical layering within each state (percussion, drones, melodic fragments) intensifies with danger level and number of enemies that have detected the player.
- Special “Identity” stingers trigger on major mutation locks, first-time intimacy, or major story revelations.

**Phoenix Voice Processing**
- Base voice is clean and human.
- Progressive real-time effects tied to meters and mutations:
  - Rising Bloodlust → subtle low-end growl and wetness
  - High Decay → increased reverb, slight pitch instability, breathiness
  - Major permanent mutations → distinct permanent voice filters (player can preview in the mutation menu)
- Internal monologue voices (Scientist, Survivor, Hungry One, Witness) each have unique EQ and spatialization so the player can distinguish them instantly.

**Zombie Vocalization System**
- Standard undead have a large library of wet, non-linguistic sounds.
- Conscious undead (including Phoenix) can speak, but their vocalizations change with state:
  - Calm → near-human
  - Hungry → words remain clear but undertone becomes animalistic
  - Uncontrolled → language breaks down into roars, clicks, and wet snarls
- Different conscious undead NPCs have unique vocal signatures so players can recognize Riven, Marrow, Iris, etc. by sound alone.

**Environmental & Story Audio**
- Important audio logs are spatially placed and can be partially obscured by weather or distance.
- Certain blood mixtures and cannabis strains temporarily alter the player’s audio perception (e.g., heightened heartbeat of nearby living creatures, muted zombie sounds, etc.).

---

### 3.17.2 Full UI/UX Specification

**HUD Philosophy:** Minimal when safe, informative when threatened. Never covers the beauty or horror of the world unnecessarily.

**Always-Visible Elements (top-left stack):**
- Bloodlust meter (segmented: Calm / Hungry / Uncontrolled thresholds clearly marked)
- Decay meter (dual bar: Surface / Deep)
- Current Disguise strength (small icon + percentage or shield graphic)
- Quick-use slots (4–6 slots for cannabis, blood vials, tools)

**Contextual Elements:**
- Stamina bar only appears when sprinting, climbing, or in combat
- Sanity indicator only appears when below 60% or when hallucinations begin
- Noise meter appears during stealth sections
- Enemy detection eyes / scent icons appear when relevant

**Inventory System**
- Hybrid weight + slot system.
- Base carry capacity is low; backpacks and certain mutations increase it.
- Quick-access radial wheel (hold a button) for medical, cannabis, and blood items.
- Items can be marked as “Favorite” for the radial wheel.
- Visual degradation shown on tools and clothing.

**Key Screens**
- **Blood Alchemy Screen:** Drag-and-drop mixing interface with real-time preview of effects and risk.
- **Cannabis Journal:** Tracks strains, phenotypes, and personal notes.
- **Identity Screen:** Shows current permanent mutations, voice preview, and how NPCs currently perceive Phoenix.
- **Relationship Screen:** Grid of major NPCs with Trust / Affinity bars and last significant memory.

**Feedback Rules**
- Every major state change (Bloodlust threshold, disguise break, mutation lock-in) has both visual and audio confirmation.
- Recipe discovery and new strain identification use satisfying, readable pop-ups that can be reviewed later in the journal.

---

### 3.17.3 Economy & Barter System

**No universal currency.** Everything is barter + reputation.

**Two Distinct Markets**

1. **Human Survivor Markets**
   - Value: Clean water, medicine, ammunition, functional tools, information, high-quality cannabis (especially calming strains), and “proof of control” (e.g., demonstrating low Bloodlust).
   - Strongly dislike visible mutations and recent feeding evidence.
   - Prices and available stock improve significantly with high Human reputation.

2. **Conscious Undead Network**
   - Value: Specific blood types (especially rare or fresh human), Lab-Grade samples, advanced cannabis extracts, mutation knowledge, and safe shelter locations.
   - More accepting of visible mutations.
   - Will trade unique permanent mutation catalysts and rare blood recipes that humans will never offer.

**Reputation Gates**
- Certain items and dialogue options only appear at specific reputation thresholds.
- Extreme negative reputation with one side can lock the player out of entire encampments.

**Special Trade Goods**
- Phoenix’s own blood (in vials) can be sold or gifted — high risk, high reward.
- Information (map knowledge, locations of other subjects, lab secrets) is a valuable trade item.
- Iris (Subject 15) runs the best cannabis-focused trading post among the conscious undead.

---

### 3.17.4 Map, Navigation & Fast Travel

**Map Revelation**
- The map starts almost completely fogged.
- Exploration reveals terrain permanently.
- Important POIs (safehouses, grow sites, other subjects’ locations, story-critical sites) can be manually pinned or automatically marked when discovered.
- Certain blood mixtures and cannabis strains can temporarily reveal nearby POIs or enemy concentrations on the map.

**Fast Travel Rules**
- Fast travel is only available between **secured safehouses** the player has fully claimed and upgraded to a minimum security level.
- Cost: A small amount of time passage + a mild Bloodlust increase (the body does not like sudden long-distance displacement).
- Risk: Very low chance of a “travel encounter” if the player’s Bloodlust is high or if they are heavily mutated (ambush on arrival).
- No fast travel while Wanted by a major faction or during certain story crises.

**Additional Navigation Tools**
- Phoenix can craft simple trail markers or scent markers.
- Animal companions with the Scout role can reveal nearby map sections.
- Late-game mutations can unlock limited passive map awareness (e.g., sensing other conscious undead at long range).


### 3.17.5 Difficulty Modes & Assist Systems

**Difficulty Presets**

| Preset     | Bloodlust Rise | Resource Abundance | Enemy Aggression | Disguise Forgiveness | Combat Timing | Intended Feel                     |
|------------|----------------|--------------------|------------------|----------------------|---------------|-----------------------------------|
| Story      | Very Slow      | High               | Low              | High                 | Generous      | Narrative focus, low punishment   |
| Normal     | Standard       | Standard           | Standard         | Standard             | Standard      | Intended experience               |
| Hard       | Fast           | Low                | High             | Low                  | Tight         | Demanding but fair                |
| Nightmare  | Very Fast      | Very Low           | Very High        | Very Low             | Very Tight    | Survival horror pressure          |

**Granular Assists (can be mixed freely)**
- Combat timing windows (parry / dodge)
- Bloodlust rise rate multiplier
- Decay rate multiplier
- Resource drop rate & plant yield
- Enemy detection range
- Aim / lock-on assist
- Crafting recipe hint strength
- Unlimited quick-save (or restricted)

**Achievement & Ending Interaction**
- Most achievements remain available on all difficulties.
- A small set of “Purist” achievements require Normal or higher with no assists.
- Endings are **never** locked behind difficulty or assists. The story always respects player choice over mechanical challenge.

---

### 3.17.6 Game Over / Failure States

**Standard Death**
- When health reaches zero, the screen desaturates, Phoenix collapses, and the player is given the options:
  - Load last save
  - Load last safehouse rest
  - Quit to main menu
- No forced progress loss beyond the usual save discipline.

**Uncontrolled Consequences (Non-Lethal)**
- Going fully Uncontrolled in front of key NPCs (Marcus, Lena, Riven, major faction leaders) permanently damages or destroys those relationships.
- Witnesses may spread information, raising Wanted level with human factions or attracting hostile conscious undead.

**Long-Term Failure States (Rare but Possible)**
- Complete and permanent loss of all known cannabis genetics (if the player’s last plants and seeds are destroyed and no backup exists).
- Becoming permanently hostile to both major human factions and the conscious undead network at the same time (extreme isolation run).
- These states do not end the game but dramatically change the experience and available endings.

**Optional Permadeath Mode**
- Available from the start as a separate toggle (“Iron Phoenix” mode).
- Death is final for that save file.
- Unlocks a unique achievement and a special New Game+ flag.

---

### 3.17.7 New Game+ / Replayability

**What Carries Over (Player Choice on NG+ Start)**
- All discovered blood recipes and cannabis genetics
- Permanent mutations (with option to reset)
- Character appearance unlocks
- Relationship memory flags (NPCs remember previous-playthrough major events in limited dialogue)
- Map knowledge and safehouse upgrades (optional)

**What Resets**
- Story progress
- Inventory
- Current meters
- World simulation state

**New Game+ Exclusive Content**
- New dialogue lines acknowledging the previous cycle
- One additional permanent mutation slot
- Harder variants of certain bosses / set-pieces
- A new “Echo” difficulty modifier that can be enabled (enemies gain limited versions of the player’s previous mutations)

**Challenge Run Support**
- Built-in modifiers (no cannabis, no blood mixing, pacifist, etc.) that can be combined and award special achievements.

---

### 3.17.8 Tutorial & Onboarding Flow (First 60–90 Minutes)

The game never pauses for a traditional checklist. Teaching is layered and contextual.

**Minute 0–10:** Opening cinematic + first Bloodlust spike + first cannabis use (teaches meters through necessity).  
**Minute 10–25:** Safe alley exploration → Multi-Stick crafting → basic scavenging → first disguise attempt (zombie meat).  
**Minute 25–45:** First human encounter (Marcus) → dialogue + reputation introduction → first blood extraction.  
**Minute 45–70:** First safehouse claiming → simple blood mix → first grow opportunity or seed finding → day/night cycle observed.  
**Minute 70–90:** Short directed journey that forces use of stealth, combat, and meter management in combination, ending with the radio message that points toward the Lab.

Every system is introduced by a concrete need rather than a popup wall of text. Optional “Codex” entries fill in deeper explanation for players who want it.

---

### 3.17.9 Performance & World Streaming Strategy

**World Structure**
- Semi-open world divided into discrete region scenes (City, Forest, Industrial, Lab, etc.).
- Only the current region + immediately adjacent transition zones are fully loaded.
- Distant regions exist as simplified collision + far LOD + audio stubs.

**Streaming Rules**
- Seamless doors / path transitions trigger background loading of the next region.
- Safehouse interiors are separate streamed scenes for memory efficiency.
- Plant simulation is active only in the current region and any region containing a player-owned grow operation (those are kept in a lightweight “background sim” state).

**AI Budget**
- Maximum active detailed AI agents: ~25–30 at once.
- Beyond that, enemies enter a simplified “horde” state (cheaper behavior tree).
- Conscious undead and named NPCs always receive full AI priority.

**Plant & World Sim Limits**
- Active individual plant simulation capped per region.
- Excess plants switch to statistical yield mode until the player returns.

---

### 3.17.10 Photo Mode / Cinematic Tools

**Access:** Available at almost any time (including combat via pause) except during critical scripted sequences.

**Features**
- Free camera with wide range of motion and field-of-view control
- Pose library for Phoenix (including mutation-specific poses)
- Expression and hand-gesture controls
- Time of day and weather override (temporary)
- Depth of field, filters, vignette, chromatic aberration
- Hide HUD / hide Phoenix / hide companions toggles
- Stickers and frame options (some unlocked through story and achievements)
- Ability to trigger limited facial animations and subtle idle breathing/mutation effects while photographing

**Sharing:** Screenshots saved to a dedicated folder and (optionally) to the platform’s native sharing system. Support for short cinematic camera paths (simple keyframe tool) for more advanced users.

1. **Audio & Music Design**
   - Dynamic music system that reacts to Bloodlust level, disguise state, and danger.
   - Voice processing for Phoenix (how her voice changes with Decay and mutations).
   - Detailed sound design for feeding, blood mixing, cannabis use, and disguise application.

2. **UI / HUD / UX Design**
   - Exact HUD layout (how Bloodlust, Decay, Sanity, disguise strength, and quick-use cannabis/blood slots are displayed).
   - Inventory management (weight system, categories, quick-wheel, examination screens).
   - Menu flow, map screen, relationship screens, and mutation overview screen.

3. **Tutorialization & First-Hour Onboarding**
   - How the game teaches the unusual premise and systems without heavy text dumps.
   - Protected tutorial space vs. immediate danger balance.

4. **Economy & Trading System**
   - What can be traded, with whom, and how reputation and visible mutations affect prices and available goods.
   - Barter vs. currency (if any).

5. **Fast Travel & Map System**
   - How fast travel is unlocked and restricted.
   - What information the map shows (and what it deliberately hides).

6. **Fail States & Game Over Conditions**
   - What actually constitutes a “game over” (permanent death vs. severe consequences).
   - Permadeath option? Corruption of saves? Soft failure states?

7.**Detailed Designs for the Requested Systems**


**Carry-Over Options (Player chooses at NG+ start)**
- Permanent mutations (keep, reset, or keep with +1 extra slot)
- All discovered blood recipes and cannabis genetics/strains
- Character creator unlocks and visual presets
- Map knowledge and claimed safehouses (optional)
- Relationship memory (NPCs receive limited “I remember a version of you” dialogue)
- Codex entries and lore

**Always Resets**
- Story flags and act progress
- Inventory and resources
- Current meters
- World simulation (plants, animal populations, faction states)

**New Game+ Exclusive Content**
- New permanent mutation category: “Echo Mutations” (only available in NG+) that reference the previous cycle’s dominant playstyle.
- Additional dialogue across all major NPCs that acknowledges the player’s previous ending and major choices.
- New challenge modifier: “Heavy Veins” (all blood mixtures have stronger effects but faster tolerance and higher mutation risk).
- New late-game blood type that only appears in NG+.
- Achievement-linked starting modifiers (e.g., starting with a rare strain if the player unlocked “Gardener of the Apocalypse” in the previous run).

**Challenge Run Support**
- Built-in selectable modifiers: No Cannabis, No Blood Mixing, Pacifist, Iron Phoenix (permadeath), Flesh Only (no tools), etc.
- Modifiers can be combined. Completing the game with certain combinations unlocks unique titles and visual auras for the character creator.

---

### 3.17.11 Weather System – Full Mechanical Depth

| Weather     | Disguise Effect                  | Blood Freshness | Cannabis Growth          | Stamina & Movement       | AI Behavior                          |
|-------------|----------------------------------|-----------------|--------------------------|--------------------------|--------------------------------------|
| Clear       | Normal                           | Normal          | Normal                   | Normal                   | Normal                               |
| Rain        | Washes away external blood/meat disguise quickly | Spoils faster   | Boosts growth, risk of mold if not covered | Slight stamina drain when soaked | Reduces scent detection, increases auditory detection |
| Heavy Storm | Almost impossible to maintain external disguise | Spoils very fast | High growth but high damage risk | Significant movement penalty | Zombies more aggressive, humans seek shelter |
| Fog         | Strong bonus to visual stealth   | Normal          | Slight negative          | Normal                   | Greatly reduced visual AI range, scent becomes primary |
| Heatwave    | Faster disguise degradation from sweat | Spoils faster   | Stress on plants, needs more water | Increased stamina drain  | Higher Bloodlust rise rate for player and conscious undead |
| Cold / Frost| Slower disguise degradation      | Lasts longer    | Growth slows or stops    | Stamina drain if poorly clothed | Reduced zombie activity, humans more desperate |

Additional rules:
- Weather is regional and can be different in distant zones.
- Certain permanent mutations grant weather resistances or bonuses.
- Player can build weather-protected grow rooms and drying shelters to mitigate effects.

---

### 3.17.12 Inventory Weight & Encumbrance

**System Type:** Hybrid weight + soft slot limits.

- Every item has a weight value.
- Phoenix has a base carry capacity (low at the start).
- Exceeding 70% capacity → mild stamina penalty.
- Exceeding 100% → severe stamina penalty, reduced movement speed, and inability to sprint or climb.
- Exceeding 130% → very slow movement and eventual forced item drop.

**Capacity Increases**
- Backpack progression (crafted and found)
- Specific permanent mutations (“Beast Backbone”, “Hollow Frame”, etc.)
- Animal companions with the Hauler role (each hauler animal adds a significant shared capacity pool)
- Temporary blood mixtures that increase carry strength

**Quality of Life**
- Quick-drop heaviest items
- “Move to storage” when near a claimed safehouse or animal pen
- Visual and audio feedback as the player approaches each threshold

---

### 3.17.10 Photo Mode / Cosmetics / Expression Tools (Expanded)

**Photo Mode Features**
- Free camera with wide movement, FOV, and roll control
- Full pose library (including mutation-specific and intimacy-adjacent poses that respect NSFW toggles)
- Facial expression slider + micro-expression triggers
- Hand and finger posing
- Temporary time-of-day and weather override
- Advanced post-process stack (DOF, color grading, film grain, chromatic aberration, bloom)
- Ability to freeze or slow idle animations (breathing, subtle mutation pulses, blood dripping, etc.)
- Hide HUD / companions / Phoenix options
- Simple keyframe cinematic path tool for short moving shots

**Cosmetics & Expression**
- Unlockable clothing variants, scars, tattoos, and body markings tied to story and challenges
- Mutation “styles” — players can choose alternate visual presentations for the same mechanical mutation
- Emote wheel for use in free exploration and when standing near companions/NPCs
- Mirror mode in safehouses for detailed self-inspection and posing

- **Post-Launch Roadmap & Performance Budgets:** Not yet defined.


### 3.18 Skill Trees – Full Concrete Perks

There are **seven** skill categories. Each category has three tiers (Novice → Adept → Master). Players earn Skill Points through use, quests, and story milestones.

#### 1. Stealth
- **Novice:** Quieter footsteps • Faster disguise application • Reduced scent while crouching  
- **Adept:** Disguise lasts 30% longer in rain • Ability to hold breath to suppress sound completely for a short time • Enemies take longer to investigate light noise  
- **Master:** “Ghost Step” — brief perfect silence movement • Can apply disguise while moving • Chance for enemies to ignore the player entirely if Bloodlust is low and disguise is high

#### 2. Combat
- **Novice:** Faster attack recovery • Increased grab success chance • Reduced stamina cost for heavy attacks  
- **Adept:** Limb-targeting unlock (arm/leg disable) • Bloodlust gains from combat slightly reduced • Weapons break more slowly  
- **Master:** “Feral Chain” — successful light attacks can chain into automatic follow-ups • Feeding is faster and safer • Permanent slight increase to base damage

#### 3. Herbalism / Cannabis Mastery
- **Novice:** Identify plant quality at a glance • Faster harvesting • Basic curing knowledge  
- **Adept:** Higher chance of rare phenotypes • Plants grow 20% faster in player-tended plots • Can make intermediate extracts  
- **Master:** “Green Thumb” — ability to stabilize any strain genetics • Perfect curing possible • Unique master-level edibles and resins

#### 4. Blood Alchemy
- **Novice:** Faster extraction • Basic risk preview • Can mix two bloods in the field  
- **Adept:** Reduced tolerance gain • Ability to add minor positive side-effects • Stabilizer efficiency increased  
- **Master:** “Vein Writer” — can create personal signature mixtures • One extra permanent mutation slot • Blood mixtures last significantly longer

#### 5. Animal Handling
- **Novice:** Animals calm faster • Basic commands unlocked • Reduced fear from Bloodlust  
- **Adept:** Can tame higher-tier animals • Hauler capacity bonus • Animals can be taught simple scouting routines  
- **Master:** “Pack Leader” — ability to have more active animal companions • Breeding produces stronger offspring • Animals can actively defend the player without command

#### 6. Crafting
- **Novice:** Faster crafting speed • Reduced material waste • Unlock basic quality tiers  
- **Adept:** Ability to add secondary properties to items • Repair efficiency increased • Can craft advanced Multi-Stick variants  
- **Master:** “Maker’s Mark” — crafted items gain permanent bonus • Can reverse-engineer found items • Unlock legendary-tier recipes

#### 7. Social
- **Novice:** Better first-impression dialogue options • Slight reputation gain boost • Can read basic NPC mood  
- **Adept:** Extra dialogue options with both humans and conscious undead • Trading prices improved • Can calm hostile NPCs more easily  
- **Master:** “Silver Tongue / Iron Presence” — major reputation swings possible through single conversations • Unlock unique story resolutions • Romance paths advance faster

---

### 3.19 Sleep / Dream / Nightmare System

**Fatigue Meter**  
Separate from the three core meters. Rises with time awake, heavy exertion, high Bloodlust, and injury. At high Fatigue the player suffers stamina penalties, slower reactions, and eventual forced micro-sleeps (dangerous in the open).

**Sleeping Rules**
- Can only achieve proper Long Rest in a claimed safehouse or equivalent secure location.
- Short rests (catnaps) are possible in hidden spots but give reduced benefits and higher nightmare chance.

**Dream Types (influenced by recent actions, cannabis strain, blood mixtures, and Sanity)**
- **Restorative Dream:** Best outcome. Full meter recovery + possible minor temporary buff.
- **Memory Dream:** Playable or cinematic flashback that can unlock lore or recipes.
- **Prophetic Dream:** Gives gameplay hints (location of a subject, danger warning, grow tip).
- **Nightmare:** Sanity damage, possible temporary debuff, or a short horror sequence. Higher chance when Bloodlust or Decay is high, or after feeding on humans.
- **Mutation Dream:** Only possible with unsettled permanent mutations. Can slightly alter the final expression of a mutation (visual or minor mechanical).

Players can influence dream type through the last cannabis strain smoked, recent blood mixtures, and whether they go to sleep while Calm or Hungry.

---

### 3.20 Tool Durability & Repair Ecosystem

**Durability Tiers**
- Every tool and weapon has a durability value.
- Quality levels: Crude → Standard → Refined → Masterwork → Legendary.
- Higher quality = more durability and better performance.

**Wear Factors**
- Hitting armor or bone
- Using a tool for a non-intended purpose (e.g. Multi-Stick as crowbar)
- Extreme weather

**Repair System**
- Field repair: quick, partial, requires basic materials, lowers maximum durability slightly.
- Workbench repair: full repair possible, requires appropriate materials and skill.
- Masterwork and Legendary items can only be fully repaired at high-tier stations or by certain NPCs.

**Break Consequences**
- When durability reaches zero the item breaks. Some items can be salvaged for parts; others are destroyed.
- Multi-Stick is special: it can always be reshaped back into a basic stick even if “broken,” preserving the player’s core tool philosophy.

---

### 3.21 Housing Decoration & Permanence

**Permanence Rules**
- Once a safehouse is fully claimed and reaches Security Level 2, it becomes permanent.
- Permanent safehouses persist across New Game+ if the player chooses to carry them over.
- The world will never delete player-built structures unless the player manually dismantles them or a major story event specifically targets that location.

**Decoration System**
- Wide range of placeable objects: furniture, lighting, wall decorations, grow equipment, trophies, notes, and personal items.
- Many decorations are functional (extra storage, comfort bonus, cannabis drying bonus, mood lighting that affects Sanity recovery).
- Players can create distinct “identities” for each base (laboratory, nest, greenhouse sanctuary, fortress, etc.).
- Some conscious undead and human NPCs will comment on and react to how the player has decorated their space.
- Photo Mode is particularly strong inside decorated safehouses.

**Comfort Score**
- Calculated from decorations, cleanliness, lighting, and security.
- Higher Comfort = better Long Rest results, faster Sanity recovery, and slight Bloodlust suppression while inside.

1. **Blending In with Humans and Zombies:**

   - Interact with both zombies and humans using dialogue options (from an instinctual zombie perspective or as a human trying to hold on to sanity).
2. **Cannabis/Crafting Integration:**
   - Marijuana/Cannabis will be essential for managing bloodlust, slowing decay, and helping with sanity. 
   - Crafting options for players will include:
     - **Marijuana-Related Items:** Grow cannabis, craft edibles, smoke, or create potions that stabilize your character.
     - **Weapons & Tools:** From basic wooden sticks and sharp knives to more advanced crafted gear. Craft items like shelters, rafts, and vehicles as you progress.
     - **Survival Mechanics:** Mix animal blood (or zombie blood) to create stronger versions of yourself, craft traps, and other tools to protect against both humans and zombies.
   
3. **NPC Interactions:**
   - Develop complex relationships with both human survivors and zombies. Depending on your choices, you may form alliances, engage in sexual interactions, or even betray others for survival.
   - The game will be dialogue-heavy, where conversations can lead to gaining new allies, information, or rewards. Choose wisely how you engage with NPCs.
4. **Combat Mechanics:**
   - Start with basic bites and scratches but eventually gain the ability to use makeshift weapons (sticks, rocks, etc.). 
   - Combat will be gritty, with the player learning new moves as they regain consciousness. 
   - The decay mechanic means that fights will get messier and more frantic as time goes on, and maintaining your health becomes increasingly difficult.
5. **Stealth and Disguise Systems:**
   - The key to survival is blending in, and this means you’ll have to cover yourself in blood, find fresh clothing, and create distractions to pass through danger zones undetected.
   - Use environmental elements like abandoned vehicles, alleys, and shadows to hide or escape.
6. **Environmental Design:**
   - **Urban Ruins:** Dilapidated buildings, abandoned subway stations, and city streets littered with looting and chaos.
   - **Natural Areas:** Overgrown forests, beaches, and rivers where you can find resources, craft tools, and grow cannabis.
   - **Day/Night Cycles:** Different threats appear depending on the time of day. Zombies might be more aggressive at night, while humans are more active during the day, creating an ever-present threat of detection.
7. **Character Customization:**
   - **Zombie Customization:** Customize your character’s appearance, from body shape and tattoos to personality traits. 
   - You can also customize your look and actions depending on your desired level of “human” consciousness vs. zombie instincts.
8. **Rehabilitation or Acceptance:**
   - Depending on the choices you make, you can either attempt to find a cure or simply embrace your new existence as a zombie. The storyline will allow for both outcomes, with branching paths based on your survival tactics and moral decisions.
9. **NSFW Content:**
   - Integrate mature themes naturally into the narrative, such as the protagonist’s struggles with intimacy and human connections, both with humans and fellow zombies.
   - You can make adult decisions that affect gameplay and the storyline, but the overall tone will still tie into the game’s dark humor, survival, and emotional struggle.
### Emotional Engagement:
The idea of a zombie protagonist still holding on to a sense of self allows for deep emotional exploration, from grappling with the loss of humanity to finding solace in old habits (like marijuana). The player will feel a connection to the character, balancing the horror with the bizarre, yet human-like, actions they take.

### **1. Finalize Game Design and Concept**

### **Repetitive, Engaging Mechanics**
