## Core Game Mechanics

### Character Progression
- Player character retains human consciousness and must balance survival needs
- Develop skills in crafting, foraging, and navigation
- Build relationships with both hostile and friendly NPCs
- Memory recovery system that reveals character backstory

### Survival Systems
- Hunger and thirst mechanics
- Temperature management
- Energy/stamina system
- Health and injury management
- Day/night cycle affects gameplay

### Crafting System
1. Basic Tools
   - Multi-purpose stick tool for digging, hitting, fishing, walking, defense
   - Stone tools for crafting and resource gathering
   - Plant fiber collection and processing into rope

2. Shelter Building
   - Temporary lean-to structures
   - Treehouse bases
   - Underground shelters
   - Waterproof roofing systems

3. Resource Gathering
   - Wood collection and processing
   - Stone mining and shaping
   - Plant harvesting
   - Water collection and filtration

### Stealth Mechanics
- Environmental camouflage system
- Sound-based detection
- Line of sight avoidance
- Weather effects on stealth
- Rubbing rotten meat
### Animal Companions
- Find and befriend various animals
- Training system for different abilities
- Companion loyalty and care mechanics
- Species-specific abilities and needs

### World Design
- Dynamic weather system
- Seasonal changes
- Resource distribution based on biomes
- Day/night cycle affecting creature behavior

### Character Creation
- Customizable appearance
- Starting skill selection
- Background story choices
- Starting location options

## Technical Requirements

### 2D Graphics
- Pixel art style with detailed animations
- Dynamic lighting system
- Weather effects
- Particle systems for environmental effects

### UI/UX
- Minimalist HUD
- Context-sensitive crafting menu
- Quick-access inventory system
- Dynamic tutorial system

### Audio Design
- Dynamic ambient sounds
- Weather sound effects
- Crafting sound effects
- Character movement sounds

## Development Priorities
1. Core survival mechanics
2. Crafting system
3. World generation
4. Character progression
5. AI systems
6. Polish and optimization

## Development Priorities

### 1. Core Survival Mechanics
The foundation of the game should focus on realistic survival elements. This includes managing resources like food, blood-lust, wood, stone, water, and marijuana, which is crucial for the protagonist's unique abilities. Implement systems for health, stamina, and stealth to ensure players feel the constant pressure of surviving in a hostile environment.

### 2. Crafting System
Develop a comprehensive crafting system that allows players to gather materials like hemp, things laying around or that u find, 0grass, stone, flint, berries, branches, other materials and wood to create tools, weapons, and shelter. The crafting system should be intuitive yet deep enough to encourage exploration and experimentation, with items like the versatile multi-stick and hemp-based products.

### 3. World Generation
Create a dynamic open-world environment that mixes urban decay with overgrown natural landscapes. The world should be procedurally generated to ensure replayability, featuring diverse biomes and makeshift survivor settlements that offer various challenges and opportunities for exploration.

### 4. Character Progression
Design a character progression system that reflects the protagonist's unique abilities as a zombie. This could include skill trees or ability upgrades that enhance stealth, combat, or crafting skills. Incorporate the use of marijuana as a resource to unlock or boost certain abilities.

### 5. AI Systems
Develop sophisticated AI for both human survivors and zombies. Human AI should exhibit behaviors such as looting, forming groups, or setting traps, while zombie AI should vary in aggression and intelligence levels. The AI should react dynamically to the player's actions, enhancing the game's challenge and immersion.

### 6. Polish and Optimization
Focus on refining game mechanics, graphics, and performance to ensure a smooth and engaging player experience. This includes bug fixing, optimizing load times, and enhancing visual effects to support both the NSFW elements and overall game aesthetics.

4. PROCEDURAL GENERATION SYSTEM

function generateChunk(seed) {
  return {
    terrain: generateTerrain(seed),
    resources: generateResources(seed),
    structures: generateStructures(seed),
    wildlife: generateWildlife(seed)
  };
}

function simulateZombieHorde(chunk) {
  if (chunk.noiseLevel > 0.6) {
    moveZombiesToward(chunk);
  }

  if (chunk.infectionLevel > 0.8) {
    spawnHorde(chunk);
  }
}


Horde Behavior:
Follow noise

3. ENGINE CORE (MAIN LOOP)
const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");

canvas.width = innerWidth;
canvas.height = innerHeight;

import { player } from "./systems/player.js";
import { updateWorld } from "./systems/world.js";
import { updateAI } from "./systems/ai.js";
import { craft } from "./systems/crafting.js";

const keys = {};

window.addEventListener("keydown", e => keys[e.key] = true);
window.addEventListener("keyup", e => keys[e.key] = false);

// GAME STATE
const state = {
  items: [],
  zombies: [],
  chunks: [],
  time: 0
};

// INIT WORLD
state.zombies.push({ x: 400, y: 300, state: "wander" });

function loop() {
  updatePlayer();
  updateWorld(state);
  updateAI(state);

  render();

  requestAnimationFrame(loop);
}

function updatePlayer() {
  if (keys["w"]) player.y -= player.speed;
  if (keys["s"]) player.y += player.speed;
  if (keys["a"]) player.x -= player.speed;
  if (keys["d"]) player.x += player.speed;

  player.noise = keys["w"] || keys["s"] || keys["a"] || keys["d"] ? 0.6 : 0.1;
}

loop();

5. ZOMBIE AI (REAL PERCEPTION SYSTEM LITE)
import { player } from "./player.js";

export function updateAI(state) {
  state.zombies.forEach(z => {

    const dx = player.x - z.x;
    const dy = player.y - z.y;
    const dist = Math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy);

    const smellFactor = player.smell.human * 0.8;
    const soundFactor = player.noise * 0.6;
    const sightFactor = dist < 120 ? 0.5 : 0;

    const awareness = smellFactor + soundFactor + sightFactor;

    if (awareness > 0.7) {
      z.state = "chase";
    } else {
      z.state = "wander";
    }

    if (z.state === "chase") {
      z.x += dx / dist;
      z.y += dy / dist;
    } else {
      z.x += (Math.random() - 0.5);
      z.y += (Math.random() - 0.5);
    }
  });
}


6. WORLD SYSTEM (LIGHT CHUNK SIMULATION)
export function updateWorld(state) {
  state.time += 1;

  // simple infection drift
  state.zombies.forEach(z => {
    if (Math.random() < 0.01) {
      z.state = "wander";
    }
  });
}


7. CRAFTING SYSTEM (MULTI-STICK CORE)
import { player } from "./player.js";

export function craft(item) {

  const inv = player.inventory;

  function has(x) {
    return inv.includes(x);
  }

  // SHARP STICK
  if (has("stick") && has("rock")) {
    remove("stick");
    remove("rock");
    inv.push("sharp_stick");
    return "crafted sharp stick";
  }

  // MULTI-STICK
  if (has("sharp_stick") && has("hemp")) {
    remove("sharp_stick");
    remove("hemp");
    inv.push("multi_stick_mk1");
    return "crafted multi-stick";
  }

  function remove(x) {
    const i = inv.indexOf(x);
    if (i > -1) inv.splice(i, 1);
  }
}


8. RENDER SYSTEM
function render() {
  ctx.clearRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);

  // player
  ctx.fillStyle = "blue";
  ctx.fillRect(player.x, player.y, 10, 10);

  // zombies
  state.zombies.forEach(z => {
    ctx.fillStyle = z.state === "chase" ? "red" : "green";
    ctx.fillRect(z.x, z.y, 10, 10);
  });
}


9. SAVE SYSTEM (BROWSER LOCAL)
export function saveGame(state, player) {
  localStorage.setItem("highUndeadSave", JSON.stringify({
    player,
    state
  }));
}

1. DATABASE LAYOUT (LOGICAL SCHEMA)
🧠 PLAYER TABLE
players (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  x INTEGER,
  y INTEGER,

  consciousness REAL,
  instinct REAL,
  infection REAL,

  hunger REAL,
  thirst REAL,

  created_at INTEGER
);


🧟 ZOMBIES TABLE
zombies (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  x INTEGER,
  y INTEGER,

  state TEXT,        -- wander | chase | feed
  health INTEGER,

  infection_level REAL,
  smell_strength REAL
);


🌍 WORLD CHUNKS TABLE
chunks (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  x INTEGER,
  y INTEGER,

  biome TEXT,
  infection_level REAL,
  loot_level REAL,
  noise_level REAL
);


🧱 ITEMS TABLE
items (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  name TEXT,
  type TEXT,
  durability INTEGER,

  contamination TEXT,
  weight REAL
);


🔧 RECIPES TABLE
recipes (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  name TEXT,
  inputs TEXT,   -- JSON string
  output TEXT
);


🤝 FACTIONS TABLE
factions (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  name TEXT,

  type TEXT,  -- human | zombie | cult | scavenger | hemp_collective
  hostility REAL,
  territory_control REAL
);


🧠 MEMORY / NARRATIVE TABLE
memories (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  player_id TEXT,

  fragment TEXT,
  intensity REAL
);

Good. Now we stop adding surface features and build something that actually scales.

HIGH & UNDEAD — v0.3
🧠 PERCEPTION + STEALTH + SMELL SYSTEM (CORE SIMULATION LAYER)
This is the difference between a “zombie game” and a living survival simulation.

1. WHY THIS SYSTEM MATTERS
Right now your AI only uses distance.
That’s weak.
We upgrade it to a 3-channel perception model:
Every entity (player, zombie, human, animal) now has:
👁️ Sight
👂 Sound
👃 Smell
Each channel behaves differently per faction.

2. ENTITY PERCEPTION MODEL
function getPerception(entity, target) {
  return {
    sight: calcSight(entity, target),
    sound: calcSound(entity, target),
    smell: calcSmell(entity, target)
  };
}


3. SIGHT SYSTEM 👁️
Based on:
Distance
Light exposure
Movement speed
Obstructions
function calcSight(e, t) {
  const dx = t.x - e.x;
  const dy = t.y - e.y;
  const dist = Math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy);

  let score = 1 - (dist / 300);

  if (t.isMoving) score += 0.2;
  if (t.isCrouching) score -= 0.4;
  if (e.isNight) score -= 0.3;

  return clamp(score, 0, 1);
}


4. SOUND SYSTEM 👂
Everything generates noise.
Action
Noise Level
Walking
0.2
Running
0.6
Fighting
0.9
Crafting
0.4
Falling
0.7
function calcSound(e, t) {
  const dx = t.x - e.x;
  const dy = t.y - e.y;
  const dist = Math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy);

  let noise = t.noiseLevel || 0;

  let score = noise - (dist / 400);

  return clamp(score, 0, 1);
}


5. SMELL SYSTEM 👃 (KEY FEATURE)
This is what makes your zombie game UNIQUE.
Every entity has a Smell Profile:
player.smell = {
  human: 0.6,
  rot: 0.4,
  blood: 0.2,
  cannabis: 0.1
};


Zombie smell logic:
function calcSmell(zombie, target) {
  let score = 0;

  score += target.smell.human * 1.0;   // humans attract zombies
  score -= target.smell.rot * 0.8;     // rot hides you
  score += target.smell.blood * 1.2;   // blood = HIGH aggression trigger

  return clamp(score, 0, 1);
}


Humans smell logic:
function humanSmell(human, target) {
  let score = 0;

  if (target.smell.rot > 0.5) score += 1; // "that's infected"
  if (target.smell.human > 0.7) score -= 0.2;

  return clamp(score, 0, 1);
}


6. CANNABIS IN STEALTH SYSTEM 🌿
Now we integrate your core mechanic properly.
Cannabis affects smell:
Effect
Result
Smoke
masks human smell temporarily
Oil
reduces detection radius
Fiber
irrelevant (crafting only)
function applyCannabisSmoke(player) {
  player.smell.human *= 0.3;
  player.smell.rot += 0.2;
}

🔥 This makes cannabis a stealth tool, not just a consumable.

7. ZOMBIE AI UPGRADE 🧟
We replace basic distance AI with full perception logic.
function updateZombie(z) {
  const p = world.player;
  const perception = getPerception(z, p);

  const alertness =
    perception.sight * 0.4 +
    perception.sound * 0.3 +
    calcSmell(z, p) * 0.7;

  if (alertness > 0.6) {
    z.state = "chase";
  } else {
    z.state = "wander";
  }

  if (z.state === "chase") {
    moveToward(z, p);
  } else {
    wander(z);
  }
}


8. STEALTH SYSTEM (PLAYER SIDE)
Player is now evaluated every frame:
function updateStealth(player) {
  player.visibility =
    (player.isMoving ? 0.5 : 0.2) +
    (player.isCrouching ? -0.3 : 0) +
    player.smell.human * 0.6 +
    player.smell.rot * -0.4;
}


9. GAMEPLAY RESULT (WHAT YOU NOW HAVE)
You now have a system where:
🧟 Zombies:
Track smell first
Hear second
See last
👤 Humans:
See first
Hear second
Smell infection instantly
🌿 Cannabis:
Becomes tactical stealth modifier
Can mask identity temporarily
Has tradeoffs (risk vs safety)

10. EMERGENT GAMEPLAY (THIS IS IMPORTANT)
This system creates situations like:
You smell too human → zombies hunt you
You smell too rotten → humans shoot you
You smoke cannabis → zombies lose track of you but humans get suspicious
You bleed → everything comes for you
You stay still → you become invisible-ish
This is the foundation of emergent survival gameplay, not scripted encounters.

HIGH & UNDEAD — v0.4
🧠 CONSCIOUSNESS + EVOLUTION + FACTION ALIGNMENT SYSTEM
This is the identity engine of your game.

1. CORE IDEA SHIFT
Right now:
You are a player surviving zombies
After this system:
You are a shifting biological entity evolving between “human cognition” and “zombie instinct” inside a persistent ecosystem.

2. CONSCIOUSNESS MODEL
We replace “leveling” entirely.
Player state becomes:
PlayerState = {
  consciousness: 0,   // mind clarity
  instinct: 0,        // zombie behavior
  memory: 0,          // retained identity
  empathy: 0,         // human connection
  aggression: 0       // combat tendency
};


3. STATE BALANCE SYSTEM
These values constantly shift based on behavior:
Examples:
Eating human flesh → instinct ↑, empathy ↓
Smoking cannabis → aggression ↓, memory ↑
Sleeping safely → consciousness ↑
Killing humans → empathy collapse
Staying near humans → memory stabilizes

4. EVOLUTION STATES
Instead of levels, you unlock biological archetypes:
Feral Zombie
↓
Hungry Walker
↓
Aware Undead
↓
Thinking Host
↓
Symbiotic Hybrid
↓
Conscious Entity
↓
Post-Human Intelligence

Each stage changes gameplay mechanics.

5. TRANSFORMATION ENGINE
function updateEvolution(player) {
  const c = player.consciousness;
  const i = player.instinct;

  if (i > 0.8 && c < 0.2) player.form = "Feral Zombie";
  if (i > 0.5 && c > 0.3) player.form = "Hungry Walker";
  if (c > 0.5) player.form = "Aware Undead";
  if (c > 0.7 && player.memory > 0.6) player.form = "Symbiotic Hybrid";
}


6. MEMORY SYSTEM 🧠
This is where story becomes systemic.
player.memoryFragments = [
  "hospital lights",
  "family voice",
  "running through streets"
];

Improves consciousness
Unlocks dialogue options
Enables human disguise behavior
Reduces instinct aggression spikes

8. DUAL IDENTITY SYSTEM 🧟👤
This is your signature mechanic.
You maintain TWO simultaneous identities:
player.identity = {
  humanMask: 0.7,
  zombieMask: 0.4
};


Behavior Effects:
High humanMask:
Humans trust you
Zombies detect you easier
High zombieMask:
Zombies ignore you
Humans shoot on sight

9. DISGUISE LOGIC
function updateDisguise(player) {
  if (player.smell.rot > 0.6) {
    player.identity.zombieMask += 0.2;
  }

  if (player.wearingHumanClothes) {
    player.identity.humanMask += 0.3;
  }

  if (player.coveredInBlood) {
    player.identity.zombieMask += 0.4;
  }
}


10. CANNABIS SYSTEM UPGRADE 🌿

Result

lowers instinct spikes

stabilizes memory

heals rot decay

Consciousness Stabilizer:
function smokeCannabis(player) {
  player.instinct *= 0.7;
  player.aggression *= 0.6;
  player.memory += 0.1;
}


11. EMERGENT GAMEPLAY OUTCOMES
This system creates real survival psychology:
Examples:
You act too human → zombies ignore you, humans suspect you
You act too zombie → humans hunt you, zombies follow you
You balance both → you become a hybrid infiltrator
You lose memory → you become a pure predator
You regain memory → you unlock story + alliances

12. FACTION INTERACTION AI
NPCs now evaluate you like this:
function evaluatePlayer(npc, player) {
  const trust =
    player.identity.humanMask * npc.humanBias +
    player.identity.zombieMask * npc.fearFactor;

  if (trust > 0.6) return "ally";
  if (trust > 0.3) return "neutral";
  return "hostile";
}

High consciousness players stabilize regions
The world reacts to YOU.

Your move — this is now a full simulation architecture.
You've assembled enough ideas that this is no longer just a game concept—it's a full game universe. The strongest aspect isn't the NSFW angle; it's the role reversal where the player is an intelligent zombie trying to survive in a world that fears them while also avoiding mindless zombies. That's a genuinely distinctive survival premise.
For a browser game, though, I'd recommend not using Unity if your target is HTML5.
Recommended Tech Stack
For a modern browser-first game:
Engine: Babylon.js
Language: TypeScript
Physics: Havok Physics or Ammo.js
Rendering: WebGPU (fallback to WebGL2)
Networking: Colyseus
Database: Supabase
Audio: Howler.js
Terrain: Procedural chunk generation
AI: Behavior Trees + GOAP + LLM-assisted NPC dialogue
Storage: IndexedDB + cloud saves
PWA: Offline support
Backend: Node.js + Express
This stack is designed specifically for web deployment and avoids many of the limitations of Unity WebGL builds.

Long-Term Progression
The player eventually chooses a path:
🧟 The Apex Zombie
Fully embrace mutation.
🧬 The Cure
Search for a way to become human again.
👑 The Undead King/Queen
Unite intelligent zombies into a civilization.
🌿 The Gardener
Restore nature and rebuild communities.
⚖ The Mediator
Unite humans and conscious zombies into a fragile coexistence.

Browser Performance Strategy
To make a game of this scale practical in HTML5:
Infinite chunk-based world streaming.
Procedural generation using deterministic seeds.
Dynamic loading/unloading of terrain and assets.
Object pooling for NPCs and items.
Instanced rendering for vegetation.
Level-of-detail (LOD) models.
Web Workers for background simulation.
Progressive asset loading.
Save only world changes ("diffs") instead of the entire world state.
This approach allows a browser-based game to feel much larger than a traditional static map while keeping performance manageable.
This has the beginnings of a very ambitious game design. I think the biggest opportunity is to narrow the initial scope while designing the architecture so it can eventually grow into your long-term vision.

Given everything you've described, the project is best thought of as a long-term, systems-driven sandbox RPG. The unique combination of playing as a conscious zombie, dual stealth against humans and zombies, deep primitive crafting, living AI simulation, and hemp/cannabis as an integrated survival resource gives it a distinct identity compared with survival games that simply put the player in the role of another human survivor.

## Core Game Mechanics

### Character Progression
- Player character retains human consciousness and must balance survival needs
- Develop skills in crafting, foraging, and navigation
- Build relationships with both hostile and friendly NPCs
- Memory recovery system that reveals character backstory

### Survival Systems
- Hunger and thirst mechanics
- Temperature management
- Energy/stamina system
- Health and injury management
- Day/night cycle affects gameplay

### Crafting System
1. Basic Tools
   - Multi-purpose stick tool for digging, hitting, fishing, walking, defense
   - Stone tools for crafting and resource gathering
   - Plant fiber collection and processing into rope

2. Shelter Building
   - Temporary lean-to structures
   - Treehouse bases
   - Underground shelters
   - Waterproof roofing systems

3. Resource Gathering
   - Wood collection and processing
   - Stone mining and shaping
   - Plant harvesting
   - Water collection and filtration

### Stealth Mechanics
- Environmental camouflage system
- Sound-based detection
- Line of sight avoidance
- Weather effects on stealth
- Rubbing rotten meat
### Animal Companions
- Find and befriend various animals
- Training system for different abilities
- Companion loyalty and care mechanics
- Species-specific abilities and needs

### World Design
- Dynamic weather system
- Seasonal changes
- Resource distribution based on biomes
- Day/night cycle affecting creature behavior

### Character Creation
- Customizable appearance
- Starting skill selection
- Background story choices
- Starting location options

## Technical Requirements

### 2D Graphics
- Pixel art style with detailed animations
- Dynamic lighting system
- Weather effects
- Particle systems for environmental effects

### UI/UX
- Minimalist HUD
- Context-sensitive crafting menu
- Quick-access inventory system
- Dynamic tutorial system

### Audio Design
- Dynamic ambient sounds
- Weather sound effects
- Crafting sound effects
- Character movement sounds

## Development Priorities
1. Core survival mechanics
2. Crafting system
3. World generation
4. Character progression
5. AI systems
6. Polish and optimization

## Development Priorities
1. Core survival mechanics
2. Crafting system
3. World generation
4. Character progression
5. AI systems
6. Polish and optimization

## Development Priorities

### 1. Core Survival Mechanics
The foundation of the game should focus on realistic survival elements. This includes managing resources like food, blood-lust, wood, stone, water, and marijuana, which is crucial for the protagonist's unique abilities. Implement systems for health, stamina, and stealth to ensure players feel the constant pressure of surviving in a hostile environment.

### 2. Crafting System
Develop a comprehensive crafting system that allows players to gather materials like hemp, things laying around or that u find, 0grass, stone, flint, berries, branches, other materials and wood to create tools, weapons, and shelter. The crafting system should be intuitive yet deep enough to encourage exploration and experimentation, with items like the versatile multi-stick and hemp-based products.

### 3. World Generation
Create a dynamic open-world environment that mixes urban decay with overgrown natural landscapes. The world should be procedurally generated to ensure replayability, featuring diverse biomes and makeshift survivor settlements that offer various challenges and opportunities for exploration.

### 4. Character Progression
Design a character progression system that reflects the protagonist's unique abilities as a zombie. This could include skill trees or ability upgrades that enhance stealth, combat, or crafting skills. Incorporate the use of marijuana as a resource to unlock or boost certain abilities.

### 5. AI Systems
Develop sophisticated AI for both human survivors and zombies. Human AI should exhibit behaviors such as looting, forming groups, or setting traps, while zombie AI should vary in aggression and intelligence levels. The AI should react dynamically to the player's actions, enhancing the game's challenge and immersion.

### 6. Polish and Optimization
Focus on refining game mechanics, graphics, and performance to ensure a smooth and engaging player experience. This includes bug fixing, optimizing load times, and enhancing visual effects to support both the NSFW elements and overall game aesthetics.
